# Implementation Evidence: KMIPKIT-0010 Profile Conformance

**Original implementation branch**: `feature/KMIPKIT-0010-profile-conformance-implementation`
**Original implementation base**: `f3b4247789ad987c97e0874c81238e79b427eb9a` (`release/1.0.0`)
**Recovery branch**: `feature/KMIPKIT-0010-profile-conformance-implementation-refresh`
**Recovery base**: `8909653b9ed64dfc1938b7dfc655b4a08178d873` (`release/1.0.0`)
**Status**: In progress; no profile conformance claim is made.

## Authorization and scope

Implementation is authorized by the human task dispatch for KMIPKIT-0010, which directs work to proceed without approval prompts in the assigned worktree. Scope is limited to the approved feature specification, its plan/contracts/tasks, and the stated fail-closed audit rulings. Human review and approval before merge remain required.

The approved specification was merged by the maintainer in [PR #49](https://github.com/NeverWe1come/KMIPKit/pull/49), merge commit `045b97dde40e54efbc264d95c1d827c4f7d40415`. This supersedes the historical `Draft` label; it does not change feature scope or represent a separate GitHub review event.

## Independent audit rulings

| Finding | Resolution | Evidence location/status |
|---|---|---|
| H1 — Baseline target and runtime eligibility were conflated | Baseline target membership is independent of runtime availability. Missing typed rules or category-appropriate tests make runtime selection fail before I/O. The 16 unavailable mandatory fixtures independently block readiness and claims. | `spec.md` Clarifications; `contracts/profile-validation.md`; `contracts/profile-evidence.md`; `data-model.md`; generator/runtime regression tests pending. |
| H2 — Conformance-category test coverage was implicit | Exact `source_keyword` determines the needed evidence: positive, negative, behavior/deviation, discouraged-behavior absence/deviation, or usable optional-capability tests. Generic test presence is insufficient. | `spec.md` Clarifications; both profile contracts; `data-model.md`; task T003/T009/T030; category tests pending. |
| M1 — Response-validation delivery state lacked explicit cases | Preflight failures return `NotSent` with zero writes; a failure after response receipt preserves the exchange delivery state, including `ResponseStarted`. | `spec.md` Clarifications; `contracts/profile-validation.md`; task T010/T031; fake-transport cases pending. |
| M2 — Traceability tests needed strict Red/Green evidence | Traceability tests must be a signed Red commit with a recorded expected failure and a separate signed Green commit with a recorded pass; Refactor is separate when applicable. | `spec.md` Clarifications; task T030; traceability commits and command records pending. |

## Environment evidence

- The assigned worktree started clean on the requested feature branch at the stated release base.
- PR #52 (`feature/KMIPKIT-0012-registry-implementation`) remains open as a draft and modifies `crates/kmipkit-client/src/execute.rs` and `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. Work on those overlapping files is deferred until that PR merges.
- A live read-only request to `GET /repos/NeverWe1come/KMIPKit/branches/release%2F1.0.0/protection` returned `404 Branch not protected` on 2026-10-07. This is insufficient evidence for eligible readiness. No conformance or release claim is made.
- Baseline Client has 16 mandatory official fixture records marked unavailable in the pinned catalog. They remain evidence blockers and are not reconstructed.
- A parallel verification invocation transiently reported a pinned OASIS source unavailable. The four catalog source paths were then checked directly with matching pinned SHA-256 values, and standalone sequential catalog validation passed. The cause was not reproduced; no upstream source was changed.
- A parallel focused verification invocation transiently reported invalid or unsafe report input. Sequential report verification and catalog validation passed immediately afterward; the cause was not reproduced.

## Verification status

Strict catalog validation independently compares the Baseline closure with clause candidates derived from the pinned Specification HTML, and recomputes and enforces all client-profile clause/requirement closures. Baseline currently stores 897 clauses and 379 requirements. The 148 profile-specific requirements in the 15 in-scope client profiles are assigned to `KMIPKIT-0010`; base Specification requirements retain existing owners and inherited source/profile links are unchanged. The fixed target manifest, generated Rust metadata, keyword-derived verification categories, and stable report target-state integration are implemented. The deterministic report redacts sensitive assignments and PEM private-key blocks from free-form diagnostic notes, and CLI failures do not echo exception details or caller paths. The report also shows per-requirement typed mapping/category-test references and per-profile runtime availability and stable-input blockers; its Rust registry reader currently supports only the intentionally empty production registry and rejects other forms until populated-registry extraction is added. The ephemeral manifest builder and profile readiness evaluator are implemented and tested in Python, including strict same-run metadata binding, fail-closed branch-policy checks, state reconciliation, and no claim publication. The recovery branch now wires a protected release push to the exact six required jobs, current branch-protection API evidence, and same-run ephemeral readiness evaluation; repository setup and a real protected push remain unverified because the current release branch is unprotected and the `release-evidence` environment is not configured. Rust now exposes a read-only local readiness view; it counts requirement records without treating them as CI evidence, remains blocked without a current manifest, preserves lower catalog states, and cannot publish a claim. The protocol crate's production mapping registry remains empty, so no profile is runtime-selectable. A separate independently reviewed requirement-closure oracle for all client profiles is still outstanding; existing requirement IDs and dispositions are catalog-derived and are not treated as independent proof. Concrete normative rule evaluators and their executable category-appropriate tests, request/response client integration, response delivery-state tests, workspace gates, and full CI checks remain outstanding. Reviewer-owned checklists remain unchecked until a human reviewer accepts them. The 16 missing Baseline fixtures and absent branch protection remain explicit claim/readiness blockers.

### T038 — coverage measurement

The baseline protocol coverage run passed its tests but failed the approved new/protocol code line-coverage gate: `cargo llvm-cov -p kmipkit-protocol --all-features` reported `profile.rs` at 82.47% lines (588 of 713) against the 95% minimum. Focused additions exercise stable validation/readiness diagnostic codes, redacted error display variants, readiness accessors, duplicate/unknown evidence records, conditional and out-of-scope states, incomplete dependency evidence, synthetic missing metadata, resolved dependency reuse, and the existing evaluator stub. The initial 97.63% result included tests embedded in production source, which the approved coverage preflight rejects. Those tests were moved to `crates/kmipkit-protocol/tests/support/profile_unit.rs`; coverage then exposed the production-only lifecycle lines below 95%. A small private reconciliation helper made those synthetic catalog states directly testable without changing profile selection, runtime eligibility, or claim behavior. After `cargo llvm-cov clean --workspace`, the final package run reported `profile.rs` line coverage at 95.93% (424/442); the full workspace run reported 96.28% (5,979/6,210). All Rust test targets passed. The workspace coverage run emitted the existing `unexpected cfg(coverage)` warning in `crates/kmipkit-ttlv/tests/value_zeroization.rs`; it passed. No generated-code exclusion was used.

The coverage preflight initially exceeded 30 seconds on `profiles_generated.rs` (1,400,692 UTF-8 bytes) because each raw-string check copied `source[index:]`. A deterministic test double rejects slice access. For the performance comparison, `_mask_rust_non_code` was timed with `time.perf_counter()` on that same decoded input. The baseline implementation loaded from `git show 01040e2^:scripts/coverage_gate.py` did not finish within a 30-second child-process bound (30.028 seconds elapsed). The Green implementation produced an equal-length 1,400,692-character masked string in 0.242736, 0.252750, and 0.267831 seconds (median 0.252750 seconds). To reproduce, load the baseline revision or current `scripts/coverage_gate.py`, read `crates/kmipkit-protocol/src/profiles_generated.rs` once as UTF-8, and time only `_mask_rust_non_code(source)`; the baseline run is bounded at 30 seconds and the current run is repeated three times. No timing threshold is used in tests. After the fix, `python -B scripts/coverage_gate.py preflight --workspace .` completed with 48 files scanned.

`pwsh -File .\scripts\Test-DependencyPolicy.ps1` passed on reviewed host `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; pinned cargo-deny 0.20.2 checked both Cargo workspaces, refreshed RustSec to commit `b8a1a33e246a0a9a3b5f377248c41a503defec74` (2026-10-07T16:40:27+02:00), validated registered exception IDs, and left both lockfile hashes unchanged. Catalog validation passed for 1,411 source clauses and 4,026 records; generated profile metadata and coverage report both matched their pinned generators. Workspace Clippy and tests included the FFI crate; KMIPKIT-0010 changes no C symbols, ABI structures, unsafe blocks, bindings, or foreign-call paths, so no FFI surface or sanitizer change was introduced. Readiness remains diagnostic and local-only, reports a stable missing-manifest blocker, and publishes no claim. The scanner change preserves source-mask semantics while removing suffix copies. A later read-only diff review found and the follow-up Red/Green/Refactor change fixed a quoted multiword diagnostic redaction gap; no actual secret was found in the checked-in catalog.

A search of committed generated Rust, catalog, and report outputs found no CI run IDs, attempts, job conclusions, or ephemeral manifest fields.

### T042 — independent security diff review

The completed read-only Codex Security diff scan `eaa09705-1a0c-42b3-b607-b75e30a0f512` reviewed the feature range `f3b4247789ad987c97e0874c81238e79b427eb9a..ee34f2c00d9197f1997f2f08c2dbabdb1a42073a`. It reported one validated low-severity CWE-200 issue: `_SENSITIVE_ASSIGNMENT` stopped at whitespace, so `credential="alpha beta"` rendered as `credential=[redacted] beta"` in a diagnostic report. No remediation was applied during that read-only scan, and the checked-in catalog contained no known secret.

The issue was corrected in three signed commits: Red `efacd75` adds `test_report_redacts_the_complete_quoted_multword_secret_value` and fails because the unique suffix marker appears in generated Markdown; Green `f8c1ead` captures the complete quoted value, including escaped delimiters; Refactor `eba976b` names the double-quoted, single-quoted, and general value patterns. `python -m unittest tools.normative_catalog.tests.test_report` passed 30 tests (1 skipped); direct double- and single-quoted reproductions both returned only `[redacted]`; and `git diff --check` passed. The final read-only diff scan `cad04d75-df70-4410-8e99-66162b26fd52` covered `f3b4247789ad987c97e0874c81238e79b427eb9a..937e4144794081ef09208dfd2079a4f789c5183d`, reviewed all 10 prepared source rows and all 21 other changed files, and reported zero findings. Its report is `C:\Users\ramp1953\AppData\Local\Temp\codex-security-scans-52gOTH\KMIPKit\937e4144794081ef09208dfd2079a4f789c5183d_20261007T200340Z_n8ypndzo\report.md`. No code changed after the scanned head; this final evidence update changes only task/evidence documentation.

### Release refresh — deterministic profile closure correction

Red `91a6eac0` added `test_checked_in_client_profile_closures_match_current_scope`; it failed for all 18 client profiles because each stored 25 stale requirement links. Green `fa3baeee` recomputed only the stored `requirement_ids` using `compute_profile_closure`. The direct source roots and all source-clause closures remained unchanged. The removed links were one nonexistent reference to table-header clause 4.16-004 (catalogued `non_applicable`) and 24 requirements now classified `server_only` or `out_of_scope` by the release catalog; no normative requirement or product scope was invented. The new test passes, catalog validation passes (4 sources, 1,411 clauses, 4,042 records), Rust profile evidence/validation tests pass (8 tests), and `generate_profiles.py --check` plus `report.py --check` pass after deterministic regeneration. Generated Rust profile metadata and the coverage report were regenerated from the corrected catalog. The remaining documentation cleanup is recorded separately from the Red/Green commits.

#### §6.1.48 correction review

The initial QA found that clause `...-003` had a replacement character and incorrect capitalization in its atom, and that clauses `...-001`/`...-006` omitted expected client IDs. Red commits `9b602f61` and `5f5ba675` add regressions for exact atom/source fidelity and crosswalk metadata. Green commit `e90d53df` preserves the pinned U+2019 and exact lowercase source text, records those two expected IDs, and explicitly marks the server actor for clauses `...-007`–`...-009` as an inference. | Refactor — normalized source excerpt helper | `python -B -m unittest tools.normative_catalog.tests.test_validate.CatalogValidationTests.test_spec_6_1_54_set_endpoint_role_reviews_payload_actor_direction_and_scope tools.normative_catalog.tests.test_validate.CatalogValidationTests.test_spec_6_1_54_set_endpoint_role_reviews_server_only_errors_and_duplicate_reasons -v`; `python -B tools/normative_catalog/report.py --check` | PASS: extracted the orphaned HTML attribute-prefix pattern to a named test helper constant without changing source excerpts; both operation tests passed and the generated coverage report verified. |

The pinned Specification v2.1 source has SHA-256 `8bf9d914c097e98a6509aa1ffcbf03406f738066e940597aee93d0a5e07addcf`. The operation prose at lines 36138–36142 says the successful response switches client/server roles over the existing channel; Table 331's client request row is Required=Yes at lines 36165–36187 and Table 332's server response row is Required=Yes at lines 36218–36240. The §6.1.54.1 SHALL at lines 36251–36255 requires the server to return Result Reasons for operation errors and is recorded server-to-client; it creates no client request requirement. Table 333's list at lines 36278–36287 contains Permission Denied twice and Attestation Required once. Attestation Required is a Result Reason value; the extracted REQUIRED token is a false positive, not a modal obligation. No direct official Set Endpoint Role test case or fixture is linked by the checked-in catalog.

The existing tag element `KMIPKIT-ELEM-TAG-420151` identifies Endpoint Role but does not state the Table 331 operation-specific Required=Yes rule. The client request body row is therefore recorded as a source-only gap proposing `KMIPKIT-REQ-SPEC-6.1.54-001`; this proposal has no stable catalog requirement ID or crosswalk. The Table 332 response body row retains server actor and server-to-client direction for client response handling. The Table 331/332 header-derived clause candidates remain `non_applicable` and are not repurposed as body-row requirements.

`KMIPKIT-DISC-052` remains open with no decision: successful role switching in §6.1.54 conflicts with the approved client-initiated-only 1.0 boundary, while server-initiated §6.2.5 remains a 1.1 exclusion. This slice makes no claim that the client supports post-switch server behavior or that the full operation is in the 1.0 profile. The independent profile oracle remains partial; global crosswalk and closure failures above block profile readiness. No CI run covers these unpushed commits; the last recorded predecessor CI run `38162579759` failed Script contracts on Ubuntu, Windows, and macOS.

Fixture totals after this slice are 230 requirement crosswalks, 621 source reviews, 160 source-only gaps, and 130 proposals. `specification/catalog/coverage-report.md` was regenerated from the catalog; no generated output was edited by hand.

The pinned KMIP Specification v2.1 §6.1.57 Validity Date row says passively that if the date is omitted, current date/time is assumed; it names no actor. `KMIPKIT-REQ-SPEC-6.1.57-006` remains `role=unspecified`, `direction=client_to_server`, `scope_state=client_1_0`, and in feature `KMIPKIT-0042`. The fixture keeps the source actor unresolved and uses `scope_disposition=client_1_0` only for the approved KMIPKit request/API serialization feature scope. Its `expected_client_requirement_ids`, requirement crosswalk membership, and profile membership remain empty. Requirement semantics, actor/scope fields, crosswalk/profile membership, runtime behavior, public API, generated files, and pinned OASIS source remain unchanged.

### KMIPKIT-0010 — Profiles v2.1 §3 item 1 actor and direction correction (catalog clause 3-002; 2026-10-12)

Profiles v2.1 §3, item 1 (pinned HTML lines 3967–3970), requires confidentiality and integrity for communication over the security channel. The clause describes a channel property and does not identify a client or server actor or a message direction. The approved KMIPKIT-0010 FR-004 closure expands a profile's cited section through its subclauses; it does not inherit parent clauses upward. ### KMIPKIT-0010 — Basic Authentication Suite client inclusion trace correction (catalog clause 3.1-001; 2026-10-12)

Profiles v2.1 §3.1, locator 1 (pinned HTML lines 3999–4001), says that a profile conforming to the Basic Authentication Suite SHALL use TLS to negotiate a secure channel. ### KMIPKIT-0010 — Profiles v2.1 §§4.1 and 4.1.2 test-only scope and unresolved actor correction (2026-10-12)

Profiles v2.1 §§4.1 and 4.1.2 describe variations and setup/teardown for successful Profile test cases, rather than a general production-client protocol contract. In §§4.1.2.1–.5 the source does not consistently identify which endpoint provides a variation; the Authentication request MAY in particular names a request but not its sender. Those rows remain actor-, direction-, and applicability-unclear and are not added to client crosswalks. Sections 4.1.2.8–.9 describe Interop Begin/End around tests; the pinned Profiles text at lines 4498–4502 says production clients are not expected to support Interop, so those test-event rows are outside production client scope. The catalog retains the source MAY/SHALL semantics and test context. The profile closure generator now treats `role=unclear` plus `scope_state=unclear` as an unresolved blocker if such a source row enters a selected closure, without applying it as a client requirement. This preserves the source review's unresolved actor classification and closes a fail-open path in FR-004.

### KMIPKIT-0010 — Map the AES XTS Client MAY while preserving its source discrepancy (2026-10-12)

Profiles v2.1 §5.13.1 item 2 (pinned HTML lines 8990–8991) grants the AES XTS Client a profile-conditional `MAY` to support KMIP-SPEC clauses subject to a stated non-conflict condition. The source literally says “this section 5.10.1” from §5.13.1. The catalog now assigns stable label `KMIPKIT-REQ-PROF-5.13.1-002`, links it to the AES XTS Client profile and open `KMIPKIT-DISC-007`, and retains direction as unclear. The summary and review explicitly preserve the literal cross-reference without inferring its intended boundary. Implementation and positive verification remain unassigned; this mapping does not make the profile runtime-selectable or claim support.
